(5.68)

and we define

\begin{split}w\left(x,y,t\right):=&\tau_{1}\left(x\right)v_{1}\left(x_{1}\right)\cdot\ldots\cdot v_{n}\left(x_{n}\right)\phi_{1}\left(y_{1}+e_{1}+\epsilon Y_{1}\right)\cdot\ldots\cdot\phi_{M}\left(y_{M}+e_{M}+\epsilon Y_{M}\right)\\
&\times\psi^{\star}_{1}(t_{1})\cdot\ldots\cdot\psi^{\star}_{l}(t_{l}),\end{split}

(5.69)

where the setting in (
5.28
), (
5.42
),
(
5.43
), (
5.46
), (
5.64
)
and (
5.65
) has been exploited.

We also notice that
w\in C\left(\mathbb{R}^{N}\right)\cap C_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{N-l})\cap\mathcal{A}
. Moreover,
if

a=(a_{1},\dots,a_{l}):=\left(-\frac{\epsilon}{\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,_{1}},\dots,-\frac{\epsilon}{\,\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,_{l}}\right)\in(-\infty,0)^{l}

(5.70)

and
(x,y)
is sufficiently close to the origin
and
t\in(a_{1},+\infty)\times\dots\times(a_{l},+\infty)
, we have that

\displaystyle\Lambda_{-\infty}w\left(x,y,t\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mbox{\large{\wedn{a}}}}_{i}\partial^{r_{i}}_{x_{i}}+\sum_{j=1}^{M}{\mbox{\large{\wedn{b}}}}_{j}(-\Delta)^{s_{j}}_{y_{j}}+\sum_{h=1}^{l}{\mbox{\large{\wedn{c}}}}\,_{h}D^{\alpha_{h}}_{t_{h},-\infty}\right)w\left(x,y,t\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mbox{\large{\wedn{a}}}}_{i}v_{1}\left(x_{1}\right)\ldots v_{i-1}\left(x_{i-1}\right)\partial^{r_{i}}_{x_{i}}v_{i}\left(x_{i}\right)v_{i+1}\left(x_{i+1}\right)\ldots v_{n}\left(x_{n}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\phi_{1}\left(y_{1}+e_{1}+\epsilon Y_{1}\right)\ldots\phi_{M}\left(y_{M}+e_{M}+\epsilon Y_{M}\right)\psi^{\star}_{1}\left(t_{1}\right)\ldots\psi^{\star}_{l-1}\left(t_{l-1}\right)\psi^{\star}_{l}\left(t_{l}\right)

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=1}^{M}{\mbox{\large{\wedn{b}}}}_{j}v_{1}\left(x_{1}\right)\ldots v_{n}\left(x_{n}\right)\phi_{1}\left(y_{1}+e_{1}+\epsilon Y_{1}\right)\ldots\phi_{j-1}\left(y_{j-1}+e_{j-1}+\epsilon Y_{j-1}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\times(-\Delta)^{s_{j}}_{y_{j}}\phi_{j}\left(y_{j}+e_{j}+\epsilon Y_{j}\right)\phi_{j+1}\left(y_{j+1}+e_{j+1}+\epsilon Y_{j+1}\right)\ldots\phi_{M}\left(y_{M}+e_{M}+\epsilon Y_{M}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\psi^{\star}_{1}\left(t_{1}\right)\ldots\psi^{\star}_{l-1}\left(t_{l-1}\right)\psi^{\star}_{l}\left(t_{l}\right)

\displaystyle+\sum_{h=1}^{l}{\mbox{\large{\wedn{c}}}}\,_{h}v_{1}\left(x_{1}\right)\ldots v_{n}\left(x_{n}\right)\phi_{1}\left(y_{1}+e_{1}+\epsilon Y_{1}\right)\ldots\phi_{M}\left(y_{M}+e_{M}+\epsilon Y_{M}\right)\psi^{\star}_{1}\left(t_{1}\right)\ldots\psi^{\star}_{h-1}\left(t_{h-1}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\times D^{\alpha_{h}}_{t_{h},-\infty}\psi^{\star}_{h}\left(t_{h}\right)\psi^{\star}_{h+1}(t_{h+1})\ldots\psi^{\star}_{l-1}\left(t_{l-1}\right)\psi^{\star}_{l}\left(t_{l}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mbox{\large{\wedn{a}}}}_{i}\overline{{\mbox{\large{\wedn{a}}}}}_{i}\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{x}}}}\,_{i}^{r_{i}}v_{1}\left(x_{1}\right)\ldots v_{n}\left(x_{n}\right)\phi_{1}\left(y_{1}+e_{1}+\epsilon Y_{1}\right)\ldots\phi_{M}\left(y_{M}+e_{M}+\epsilon Y_{M}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\psi^{\star}_{1}(t_{1})\ldots\psi^{\star}_{l-1}(t_{l-1})\psi^{\star}_{l}(t_{l})

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=1}^{M}{\mbox{\large{\wedn{b}}}}_{j}\lambda_{j}v_{1}\left(x_{1}\right)\ldots v_{n}\left(x_{n}\right)\phi_{1}\left(y_{1}+e_{1}+\epsilon Y_{1}\right)\ldots\phi_{M}\left(y_{M}+e_{M}+\epsilon Y_{M}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\psi^{\star}_{1}(t_{1})\ldots\psi^{\star}_{l-1}\left(t_{l-1}\right)\psi^{\star}_{l}(t_{l})

\displaystyle+\sum_{h=1}^{l-1}{\mbox{\large{\wedn{c}}}}\,_{h}\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,_{\star,h}v_{1}\left(x_{1}\right)\ldots v_{n}\left(x_{n}\right)\phi_{1}\left(y_{1}+e_{1}+\epsilon Y_{1}\right)\ldots\phi_{M}\left(y_{M}+e_{M}+\epsilon Y_{M}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\psi^{\star}_{1}(t_{1})\ldots\psi^{\star}_{l-1}(t_{l-1})\psi^{\star}_{l}(t_{l})

\displaystyle+{\mbox{\large{\wedn{c}}}}\,_{l}\lambda\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,_{\star,l}v_{1}\left(x_{1}\right)\ldots v_{n}\left(x_{n}\right)\phi_{1}\left(y_{1}+e_{1}+\epsilon Y_{1}\right)\ldots\phi_{M}\left(y_{M}+e_{M}+\epsilon Y_{M}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\psi^{\star}_{1}(t_{1})\ldots\psi^{\star}_{l-1}(t_{l-1})\psi^{\star}_{l}(t_{l})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(-\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mbox{\large{\wedn{a}}}}_{i}\overline{{\mbox{\large{\wedn{a}}}}}_{i}\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{x}}}}\,_{i}^{r_{i}}+\sum_{j=1}^{M}{\mbox{\large{\wedn{b}}}}_{j}\lambda_{j}+\sum_{h=1}^{l-1}{\mbox{\large{\wedn{c}}}}\,_{h}\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,_{\star,h}+{\mbox{\large{\wedn{c}}}}\,_{l}\lambda\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,_{\star,l}\right)w(x,y,t),

thanks to (
5.28
), (
5.32
), (
5.44
)
and (
5.67
).

Consequently, making use of (
5.37
) and (
5.61
), when
(x,y)
is
near the origin and
t\in(a_{1},+\infty)\times\dots\times(a_{l},+\infty)
,
we have that

\Lambda_{-\infty}w\left(x,y,t\right)=\left(-\sum_{i=1}^{n}|{\mbox{\large{\wedn{a}}}}_{i}|\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{x}}}}\,_{i}^{r_{i}}+\sum_{j=1}^{M}{\mbox{\large{\wedn{b}}}}_{j}\lambda_{j}+\sum_{h=1}^{l-1}{\mbox{\large{\wedn{c}}}}\,_{h}\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,_{\star,h}+\lambda{\mbox{\large{\wedn{c}}}}\,_{l}\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,_{\star,l}\right)w(x,y,t)=0.

This says that
w\in\mathcal{H}
. Thus, in light of (
5.12
) we have that

0=\theta\cdot\partial^{K}w\left(0\right)=\sum_{\left|\iota\right|\leq K}{\theta_{\iota}\partial^{\iota}w\left(0\right)}=\sum_{|i|+|I|+|\mathfrak{I}|\leq K}\theta_{i,I,\mathfrak{I}}\,\partial_{x}^{i}\partial_{y}^{I}\partial_{t}^{\mathfrak{I}}w\left(0\right).

Hence, in view of (
5.52
) and (
5.69
),

\begin{split}0\,&=\,\sum_{|i|+|I|+|\mathfrak{I}|\leq K}\theta_{i,I,\mathfrak{I}}\,\partial^{i_{1}}_{x_{1}}{v}_{1}(0)\ldots\partial^{i_{n}}_{x_{n}}{v}_{n}(0)\\
&\qquad\times\partial^{I_{1}}_{y_{1}}{\phi}_{1}(e_{1}+\epsilon Y_{1})\ldots\partial^{I_{M}}_{y_{M}}{\phi}_{M}(e_{M}+\epsilon Y_{M})\,\partial_{t_{1}}^{\mathfrak{I}_{1}}\psi_{1}(0)\ldots\partial_{t_{l}}^{\mathfrak{I}_{l}}\psi_{l}(0)\\
&=\,\sum_{|i|+|I|+|\mathfrak{I}|\leq K}\theta_{i,I,\mathfrak{I}}\,\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{x}}}}\,_{1}^{r_{1}}\ldots\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{x}}}}\,_{n}^{r_{n}}\,\partial^{i_{1}}_{x_{1}}\overline{v}_{1}(0)\ldots\partial^{i_{n}}_{x_{n}}\overline{v}_{n}(0)\\
&\qquad\times\partial^{I_{1}}_{y_{1}}{\phi}_{1}(e_{1}+\epsilon Y_{1})\ldots\partial^{I_{M}}_{y_{M}}{\phi}_{M}(e_{M}+\epsilon Y_{M})\,\partial_{t_{1}}^{\mathfrak{I}_{1}}\psi_{1}(0)\ldots\partial_{t_{l}}^{\mathfrak{I}_{l}}\psi_{l}(0).\end{split}

(5.71)

Moreover, using (
3.1
), (
5.65
)
and (
5.70
),
it follows that

\displaystyle\partial^{\mathfrak{I}_{l}}_{t_{l}}\psi_{l}(0)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{+\infty}{\frac{\lambda^{j}\,\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,_{\star,l}^{j}\,\alpha_{l}j(\alpha_{l}j-1)\dots(\alpha_{l}j-\mathfrak{I}_{l}+1)(0-a_{l})^{\alpha_{l}j-\mathfrak{I}_{l}}}{\Gamma\left(\alpha_{l}j+1\right)}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{+\infty}{\frac{\lambda^{j}\,\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,_{\star,l}^{j}\,\alpha_{l}j(\alpha_{l}j-1)\dots(\alpha_{l}j-\mathfrak{I}_{l}+1)\,{\epsilon}^{\alpha_{l}j-\mathfrak{I}_{l}}}{\Gamma\left(\alpha_{l}j+1\right)\;{\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,_{l}}^{\alpha_{l}j-\mathfrak{I}_{l}}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{+\infty}{\frac{\lambda^{j}\,\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,_{\star,l}^{j}\,\alpha_{l}j(\alpha_{l}j-1)\dots(\alpha_{l}j-\mathfrak{I}_{l}+1)\,{\epsilon}^{\alpha_{l}j-\mathfrak{I}_{l}}}{\Gamma\left(\alpha_{l}j+1\right)\;{\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,_{l}}^{\alpha_{l}j-\mathfrak{I}_{l}}}}.

Accordingly, by (
5.30
), we find that

\begin{split}&\lim_{\epsilon\searrow 0}\epsilon^{\mathfrak{I}_{l}-\alpha_{l}}\partial^{\mathfrak{I}_{l}}_{t_{l}}\psi_{l}(0)=\lim_{\epsilon\searrow 0}\sum_{j=1}^{+\infty}{\frac{\lambda^{j}\,\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,_{\star,l}^{j}\,\alpha_{l}j(\alpha_{l}j-1)\dots(\alpha_{l}j-\mathfrak{I}_{l}+1)\,{\epsilon}^{\alpha_{l}(j-1)}}{\Gamma\left(\alpha_{l}j+1\right)\;{\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,_{l}}^{\alpha_{l}j-\mathfrak{I}_{l}}}}\\
&\qquad={\frac{\lambda\,\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,_{\star,l}\,\alpha_{l}(\alpha_{l}-1)\dots(\alpha_{l}-\mathfrak{I}_{l}+1)}{\Gamma\left(\alpha_{l}+1\right)\;\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,_{l}^{\alpha_{l}-\mathfrak{I}_{l}}}}={\frac{\lambda\,\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,_{l}^{\mathfrak{I}_{l}}\,\alpha_{l}(\alpha_{l}-1)\dots(\alpha_{l}-\mathfrak{I}_{l}+1)}{\Gamma\left(\alpha_{l}+1\right)}}.\end{split}

(5.72)

Hence, recalling (
5.14
), we can write (
5.71
) as

\begin{split}&0=\sum_{{|i|+|I|+|\mathfrak{I}|\leq K}\atop{|\mathfrak{I}|\leq|\overline{\mathfrak{I}}|}}\theta_{i,I,\mathfrak{I}}\,\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{x}}}}\,_{1}^{r_{1}}\ldots\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{x}}}}\,_{n}^{r_{n}}\,\partial^{i_{1}}_{x_{1}}\overline{v}_{1}(0)\ldots\partial^{i_{n}}_{x_{n}}\overline{v}_{n}(0)\\
&\qquad\qquad\times\partial^{I_{1}}_{y_{1}}{\phi}_{1}(e_{1}+\epsilon Y_{1})\ldots\partial^{I_{M}}_{y_{M}}{\phi}_{M}(e_{M}+\epsilon Y_{M})\partial^{\mathfrak{I}_{1}}_{t_{1}}\psi_{1}(0)\ldots\partial^{\mathfrak{I}_{l}}_{t_{l}}\psi_{l}(0).\end{split}

(5.73)

Moreover, we define

\Xi:=|\overline{\mathfrak{I}}|-\sum_{h=1}^{l}{\alpha_{h}}+\left|I\right|-\sum_{j=1}^{M}{s_{j}}.

Then, we
multiply (
5.73
) by
\epsilon^{\Xi}\in(0,+\infty)
, and we
send
\epsilon
to zero. In this way, we obtain from (
5.55
), used here for
h\in\{1,\ldots,l-1\}
,
(
5.72
) and (
5.73
) that

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon\searrow 0}\epsilon^{\Xi}\sum_{{|i|+|I|+|\mathfrak{I}|\leq K}\atop{|\mathfrak{I}|\leq|\overline{\mathfrak{I}}|}}\theta_{i,I,\mathfrak{I}}\,\,\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{x}}}}\,_{1}^{r_{1}}\ldots\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{x}}}}\,_{n}^{r_{n}}\,\partial^{i_{1}}_{x_{1}}\overline{v}_{1}(0)\ldots\partial^{i_{n}}_{x_{n}}\overline{v}_{n}(0)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\partial_{y_{1}}^{I_{1}}\phi_{1}\left(e_{1}+\epsilon Y_{1}\right)\ldots\partial_{y_{M}}^{I_{M}}\phi_{M}\left(e_{M}+\epsilon Y_{M}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\partial^{\mathfrak{I}_{1}}_{t_{1}}\psi_{1}(0)\ldots\partial^{\mathfrak{I}_{l}}_{t_{l}}\psi_{l}(0)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon\searrow 0}\sum_{{|i|+|I|+|\mathfrak{I}|\leq K}\atop{|\mathfrak{I}|\leq|\overline{\mathfrak{I}}|}}\epsilon^{|\overline{\mathfrak{I}}|-|\mathfrak{I}|}\theta_{i,I,\mathfrak{I}}\,\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{x}}}}\,_{1}^{r_{1}}\ldots\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{x}}}}\,_{n}^{r_{n}}\,\partial^{i_{1}}_{x_{1}}\overline{v}_{1}(0)\ldots\partial^{i_{n}}_{x_{n}}\overline{v}_{n}(0)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\epsilon^{|I_{1}|-s_{1}}\partial_{y_{1}}^{I_{1}}\phi_{1}\left(e_{1}+\epsilon Y_{1}\right)\ldots\epsilon^{|I_{M}|-s_{M}}\partial_{y_{M}}^{I_{M}}\phi_{M}\left(e_{M}+\epsilon Y_{M}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\epsilon^{\mathfrak{I}_{1}-\alpha_{1}}\partial^{\mathfrak{I}_{1}}_{t_{1}}\psi_{1}(0)\ldots\epsilon^{\mathfrak{I}_{l}-\alpha_{l}}\partial^{\mathfrak{I}_{l}}_{t_{l}}\psi_{l}(0)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{{|i|+|I|+|\mathfrak{I}|\leq K}\atop{|\mathfrak{I}|=|\overline{\mathfrak{I}}|}}\lambda\,\tilde{C}_{i,I,\mathfrak{I}}\,\theta_{i,I,\mathfrak{I}}\,\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{x}}}}\,_{1}^{r_{1}}\ldots\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{x}}}}\,_{n}^{r_{n}}\,\partial^{i_{1}}_{x_{1}}\overline{v}_{1}(0)\ldots\partial^{i_{n}}_{x_{n}}\overline{v}_{n}(0)

\displaystyle\qquad\times e_{1}^{I_{1}}\ldots e_{M}^{I_{M}}\,\left(-e_{1}\cdot Y_{1}\right)_{+}^{s_{1}-|I_{1}|}\ldots\left(-e_{M}\cdot Y_{M}\right)_{+}^{s_{M}-|I_{M}|}\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,_{1}^{\mathfrak{I}_{1}}\ldots\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,_{l}^{\mathfrak{I}_{l}},

for a suitable
\tilde{C}_{i,I,\mathfrak{I}}
. We stress that
\tilde{C}_{i,I,\mathfrak{I}}\neq 0
,
thanks also to (
5.54
),
applied here with
\omega_{j}:=1
,
\tilde{\phi}_{\star,j}:=\phi_{j}

and
e_{j}
as in (
5.68
) for any
j\in\{1,\ldots,M\}
.

Hence, recalling (
5.62
),

\begin{split}0&\,=\,\sum_{{|i|+|I|+|\mathfrak{I}|\leq K}\atop{|\mathfrak{I}|=|\overline{\mathfrak{I}}|}}C_{i,I,\mathfrak{I}}\,\theta_{i_{1},\dots,i_{n},I_{1},\dots,I_{M},\mathfrak{I}_{1},\dots,\mathfrak{I}_{l}}\;\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{x}}}}\,_{1}^{i_{1}}\ldots\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{x}}}}\,_{n}^{i_{n}}\\
&\qquad\qquad\times e_{1}^{I_{1}}\ldots e_{M}^{I_{M}}\,\left(-e_{1}\cdot Y_{1}\right)_{+}^{s_{1}-|I_{1}|}\ldots\left(-e_{M}\cdot Y_{M}\right)_{+}^{s_{M}-|I_{M}|}\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,_{1}^{\mathfrak{I}_{1}}\ldots\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,_{l}^{\mathfrak{I}_{l}}\\
&\,=\,\left(-e_{1}\cdot Y_{1}\right)_{+}^{s_{1}}\ldots\left(-e_{M}\cdot Y_{M}\right)_{+}^{s_{M}}\\
&\qquad\qquad\times\sum_{{|i|+|I|+|\mathfrak{I}|\leq K}\atop{|\mathfrak{I}|=|\overline{\mathfrak{I}}|}}C_{i,I,\mathfrak{I}}\,\theta_{i,I,\mathfrak{I}}\;\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{x}}}}\,^{i}\,e^{I}\,\left(-e_{1}\cdot Y_{1}\right)_{+}^{-|I_{1}|}\ldots\left(-e_{M}\cdot Y_{M}\right)_{+}^{-|I_{M}|}\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,^{\mathfrak{I}},\end{split}

(5.74)

for a suitable
C_{i,I,\mathfrak{I}}\neq 0
.

We observe that the equality in (
5.74
)
is valid for any choice of the free parameters
(\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{x}}}}\,,Y,\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,)

in an open subset of
\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}+\dots+p_{n}}\times\mathbb{R}^{m_{1}+\ldots+m_{M}}\times\mathbb{R}^{l}
,
as prescribed in (
5.20
), (
5.21
)
and (
5.46
).

Now, we take new free parameters
\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{y}}}}\,_{j}
with
\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{y}}}}\,_{j}\in\mathbb{R}^{m_{j}}\setminus\{0\}
for any
j=1,\ldots,M
, and perform in (
5.74
) the same change of variables done in (
5.58
), obtaining that

0=\sum_{{|i|+|I|+|\mathfrak{I}|\leq K}\atop{|\mathfrak{I}|=|\overline{\mathfrak{I}}|}}C_{i,I,\mathfrak{I}}\,\theta_{i,I,\mathfrak{I}}\;\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{x}}}}\,^{i}\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{y}}}}\,^{I}\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,^{\mathfrak{I}},

for some
C_{i,I,\mathfrak{I}}\neq 0
.

Hence,
the second identity in (
5.15
) is obtained as desired, and the proof of Lemma
5.3
in case
2

is completed.
∎

Proof of (
5.15
), case
3
.

We divide the proof of case
3
into two subcases, namely
either

there exists
h\in\{1,\ldots,l\}
such that
{\mbox{\large{\wedn{c}}}}\,_{h}\neq 0
,

(5.75)

or

{\mbox{\large{\wedn{c}}}}\,_{h}=0\,
for every
h\in\{1,\ldots,l\}
.

(5.76)

We start by dealing with the case in (
5.75
).
Up to relabeling and reordering the coefficients
{\mbox{\large{\wedn{c}}}}\,_{h}
, we can assume that

{\mbox{${\mbox{\large{\wedn{c}}}}\,_{1}\neq 0$}}.

(5.77)

Also, thanks to the assumptions given in case
3
, we can suppose that

{\mbox{${\mbox{\large{\wedn{b}}}}_{M}<0$}},

(5.78)

and, for any
j\in\{1,\ldots,M\}
, we consider
\lambda_{\star,j}
and
\tilde{\phi}_{\star,j}
as in (
5.16
).
Then, we take
\omega_{j}:=1
and
\phi_{j}
as in (
5.27
),
so that (
5.28
) is satisfied.
In particular, here we have that

\lambda_{j}=\lambda_{\star,j}\qquad{\mbox{ and }}\qquad\phi_{j}=\tilde{\phi}_{\star,j}.

(5.79)

We define

R:=\frac{1}{|{\mbox{\large{\wedn{c}}}}\,_{1}|}\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{M-1}|{\mbox{\large{\wedn{b}}}}_{j}|\lambda_{\star,j}.

(5.80)

We notice that, in light of (
5.77
),
the setting in (
5.80
) is well-defined.

Now, we fix a set of free parameters

\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,_{\star,1}\in(R+1,R+2),\dots\,\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,_{\star,l}\in(R+1,R+2).

(5.81)

Moreover, we define

\lambda_{M}\,:=\,\frac{1}{{\mbox{\large{\wedn{b}}}}_{M}}\left(-\sum_{j=1}^{M-1}{\mbox{\large{\wedn{b}}}}_{j}\lambda_{\star,j}-\sum_{h=1}^{l}|{\mbox{\large{\wedn{c}}}}\,_{h}|\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,_{\star,h}\right).

(5.82)

We notice that (
5.82
) is well-defined thanks to (
5.78
).
From (
5.80
) we deduce that

\begin{split}\sum_{h=1}^{l}&|{\mbox{\large{\wedn{c}}}}\,_{h}|\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,_{\star,h}+\sum_{j=1}^{M-1}{\mbox{\large{\wedn{b}}}}_{j}\lambda_{\star,j}\geq|{\mbox{\large{\wedn{c}}}}\,_{1}|\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,_{\star,1}-\sum_{j=1}^{M-1}|{\mbox{\large{\wedn{b}}}}_{j}|\lambda_{\star,j}\\
&>|{\mbox{\large{\wedn{c}}}}\,_{1}|R-\sum_{j=1}^{M-1}|{\mbox{\large{\wedn{b}}}}_{j}|\lambda_{\star,j}=0.\end{split}

Consequently, by (
5.78
) and (
5.82
),

\lambda_{M}>0.

(5.83)

Now, we define, for any
h\in\left\{1,\ldots,l\right\}
,

\overline{{\mbox{\large{\wedn{c}}}}\,}_{h}:=\begin{cases}\displaystyle\frac{{\mbox{\large{\wedn{c}}}}\,_{h}}{\left|{\mbox{\large{\wedn{c}}}}\,_{h}\right|}\quad&\text{ if }{\mbox{\large{\wedn{c}}}}\,_{h}\neq 0,\\
1\quad&\text{ if }{\mbox{\large{\wedn{c}}}}\,_{h}=0.\end{cases}

We notice that

\overline{{\mbox{\large{\wedn{c}}}}\,}_{h}\neq 0
for all
h\in\left\{1,\ldots,l\right\}
,

(5.84)

and

{{\mbox{\large{\wedn{c}}}}\,}_{h}\overline{{\mbox{\large{\wedn{c}}}}\,}_{h}=|{{\mbox{\large{\wedn{c}}}}\,}_{h}|.

(5.85)

Moreover,
we consider
a_{h}\in(-2,0)
, for every
h=1,\dots,l
,
to be chosen appropriately in what follows (see (
5.93
)
for a precise choice).

Now, for every
h\in\{1,\ldots,l\}
, we define

\psi_{h}(t_{h}):=E_{\alpha_{h},1}(\overline{{\mbox{\large{\wedn{c}}}}\,}_{h}\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,_{\star,h}(t_{h}-a_{h})^{\alpha_{h}}),

(5.86)

where
E_{\alpha_{h},1}
denotes the Mittag-Leffler function
with parameters
\alpha:=\alpha_{h}
and
\beta:=1
as defined
in (
3.1
). By Lemma
3.2
,
we know that

\begin{cases}D^{\alpha_{h}}_{t_{h},a_{h}}\psi_{h}(t_{h})=\overline{{\mbox{\large{\wedn{c}}}}\,}_{h}\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,_{\star,h}\psi_{h}(t_{h})\quad\text{in }\,(a_{h},+\infty),\\
\psi_{h}(a_{h})=1,\\
\partial^{m}_{t_{h}}\psi_{h}(a_{h})=0\quad\text{for any}\,m=1,\dots,[\alpha_{h}],\end{cases}

(5.87)

and we consider again the extension
\psi^{\star}_{h}
given in (
5.33
).
By Lemma A.3 in
[
CDV18
]
, we know that (
5.87
)
translates into

D^{\alpha_{h}}_{t_{h},-\infty}\psi_{h}^{\star}(t_{h})=\overline{{\mbox{\large{\wedn{c}}}}\,}_{h}\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,_{\star,h}\psi_{h}^{\star}(t_{h})\,\text{ in every interval }\,I\Subset(a_{h},+\infty).

(5.88)

Now, we
consider auxiliary parameters
\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,_{h}
,
e_{j}
and
Y_{j}

as in (
5.30
), (
5.45
) and (
5.46
).
Moreover, we introduce an additional set of free parameters

\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{x}}}}\,=(\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{x}}}}\,_{1},\ldots,\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{x}}}}\,_{n})\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}}\times\ldots\times\mathbb{R}^{p_{n}}.

(5.89)

We
let
\epsilon>0
, to be taken small
possibly depending on the
free parameters.
We take
\tau\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}+\ldots+p_{n}},[0+\infty))
such that

\tau(x):=\begin{cases}\exp\left({\,\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{x}}}}\,\cdot x}\right)&\quad\text{if }\,x\in B_{1}^{p_{1}+\ldots+p_{n}},\\
0&\quad\text{if }\,x\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}+\ldots+p_{n}}\setminus B_{2}^{p_{1}+\ldots+p_{n}},\end{cases}

(5.90)

where

\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{x}}}}\,\cdot x:=\sum_{j=1}^{n}\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{x}}}}\,_{i}\cdot x_{i}

denotes the standard scalar product.

We notice that, for any
i\in\mathbb{N}^{p_{1}}\times\ldots\times\mathbb{N}^{p_{n}}
,

\partial^{i}_{x}\tau(0)=\partial^{i_{1}}_{x_{1}}\ldots\partial^{i_{n}}_{x_{n}}\tau(0)=\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{x}}}}\,^{i_{11}}_{11}\ldots\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{x}}}}\,^{i_{1p_{1}}}_{1p_{1}}\ldots\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{x}}}}\,^{i_{n1}}_{n1}\ldots\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{x}}}}\,^{i_{np_{n}}}_{np_{n}}=\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{x}}}}\,^{i}.

(5.91)

We define

w\left(x,y,t\right):=\tau(x)\phi_{1}\left(y_{1}+e_{1}+\epsilon Y_{1}\right)\cdot\ldots\cdot\phi_{M}\left(y_{M}+e_{M}+\epsilon Y_{M}\right)\psi^{\star}_{1}(t_{1})\cdot\ldots\cdot\psi^{\star}_{l}(t_{l}),

(5.92)

where the setting in (
5.28
)
has also been exploited.

We also notice that
w\in C\left(\mathbb{R}^{N}\right)\cap C_{0}\left(\mathbb{R}^{N-l}\right)\cap\mathcal{A}
. Moreover,
if

a=(a_{1},\dots,a_{l}):=\left(-\frac{\epsilon}{\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,_{1}},\dots,-\frac{\epsilon}{\,\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,_{l}}\right)\in(-\infty,0)^{l}

(5.93)

and
\left(x,y\right)
is sufficiently close to the origin
and
t\in(a_{1},+\infty)\times\dots\times(a_{l},+\infty)
, we have that

\displaystyle\Lambda_{-\infty}w\left(x,y,t\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{j=1}^{M}{\mbox{\large{\wedn{b}}}}_{j}(-\Delta)^{s_{j}}_{y_{j}}+\sum_{h=1}^{l}{\mbox{\large{\wedn{c}}}}\,_{h}D^{\alpha_{h}}_{t_{h},-\infty}\right)w\left(x,y,t\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{M}{\mbox{\large{\wedn{b}}}}_{j}\tau(x)\phi_{1}\left(y_{1}+e_{1}+\epsilon Y_{1}\right)\ldots\phi_{j-1}\left(y_{j-1}+e_{j-1}+\epsilon Y_{j-1}\right)(-\Delta)^{s_{j}}_{y_{j}}\phi_{j}\left(y_{j}+e_{j}+\epsilon Y_{j}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\phi_{j+1}\left(y_{j+1}+e_{j+1}+\epsilon Y_{j+1}\right)\ldots\phi_{M}\left(y_{M}+e_{M}+\epsilon Y_{M}\right)\psi^{\star}_{1}\left(t_{1}\right)\ldots\psi^{\star}_{l}\left(t_{l}\right)

\displaystyle+\sum_{h=1}^{l}{\mbox{\large{\wedn{c}}}}\,_{h}\tau(x)\phi_{1}\left(y_{1}+e_{1}+\epsilon Y_{1}\right)\ldots\phi_{M}\left(y_{M}+e_{M}+\epsilon Y_{M}\right)\psi^{\star}_{1}\left(t_{1}\right)\ldots\psi^{\star}_{h-1}\left(t_{h-1}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\times D^{\alpha_{h}}_{t_{h},-\infty}\psi^{\star}_{h}\left(t_{h}\right)\psi^{\star}_{h+1}(t_{h+1})\ldots\psi^{\star}_{l}\left(t_{l}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{M}{\mbox{\large{\wedn{b}}}}_{j}\lambda_{j}\tau(x)\phi_{1}\left(y_{1}+e_{1}+\epsilon Y_{1}\right)\ldots\phi_{M}\left(y_{M}+e_{M}+\epsilon Y_{M}\right)\psi^{\star}_{1}(t_{1})\ldots\psi^{\star}_{l}(t_{l})

\displaystyle+\sum_{h=1}^{l}{\mbox{\large{\wedn{c}}}}\,_{h}\overline{{\mbox{\large{\wedn{c}}}}\,}_{h}\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,_{\star,h}\tau(x)\phi_{1}\left(y_{1}+e_{1}+\epsilon Y_{1}\right)\ldots\phi_{M}\left(y_{M}+e_{M}+\epsilon Y_{M}\right)\psi^{\star}_{1}(t_{1})\ldots\psi^{\star}_{l}(t_{l})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{j=1}^{M}{\mbox{\large{\wedn{b}}}}_{j}\lambda_{j}+\sum_{h=1}^{l}{\mbox{\large{\wedn{c}}}}\,_{h}\overline{{\mbox{\large{\wedn{c}}}}\,}_{h}\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,_{\star,h}\right)w(x,y,t),

thanks to (
5.28
) and (
5.88
).

Consequently, making use of (
5.79
), (
5.82
) and (
5.85
),
if
(x,y)
is near the origin and
t\in(a_{1},+\infty)\times\dots\times(a_{l},+\infty)
,
we have that

\Lambda_{-\infty}w\left(x,y,t\right)=\left(\sum_{j=1}^{M}{\mbox{\large{\wedn{b}}}}_{j}\lambda_{\star,j}+{\mbox{\large{\wedn{b}}}}_{M}\lambda_{M}+\sum_{h=1}^{l}|{\mbox{\large{\wedn{c}}}}\,_{h}|\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,_{\star,h}\right)w(x,y,t)=0.

This says that
w\in\mathcal{H}
. Thus, in light of (
5.12
) we have that

0=\theta\cdot\partial^{K}w\left(0\right)=\sum_{\left|\iota\right|\leq K}{\theta_{\iota}\partial^{\iota}w\left(0\right)}=\sum_{|i|+|I|+|\mathfrak{I}|\leq K}\theta_{i,I,\mathfrak{I}}\,\partial_{x}^{i}\partial_{y}^{I}\partial_{t}^{\mathfrak{I}}w\left(0\right).

From this and (
5.92
), we obtain that

0=\sum_{|i|+|I|+|\mathfrak{I}|\leq K}\theta_{i,I,\mathfrak{I}}\,\partial^{i}_{x}\tau(0)\partial^{I_{1}}_{y_{1}}{\phi}_{1}(e_{1}+\epsilon Y_{1})\ldots\partial^{I_{M}}_{y_{M}}{\phi}_{M}(e_{M}+\epsilon Y_{M})\,\partial_{t_{1}}^{\mathfrak{I}_{1}}\psi_{1}(0)\ldots\partial_{t_{l}}^{\mathfrak{I}_{l}}\psi_{l}(0).

(5.94)

Moreover, using (
5.86
) and (
5.93
),
it follows that, for every
\mathfrak{I}_{h}\in\mathbb{N}

\displaystyle\partial^{\mathfrak{I}_{h}}_{t_{h}}\psi_{h}(0)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{+\infty}{\frac{\overline{{\mbox{\large{\wedn{c}}}}\,}_{h}^{j}\,\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,_{\star,h}^{j}\,\alpha_{h}j(\alpha_{h}j-1)\dots(\alpha_{h}j-\mathfrak{I}_{l}+1)(0-a_{h})^{\alpha_{h}j-\mathfrak{I}_{h}}}{\Gamma\left(\alpha_{h}j+1\right)}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{+\infty}{\frac{\overline{{\mbox{\large{\wedn{c}}}}\,}_{h}^{j}\,\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,_{\star,h}^{j}\,\alpha_{h}j(\alpha_{h}j-1)\dots(\alpha_{h}j-\mathfrak{I}_{h}+1)\,{\epsilon}^{\alpha_{h}j-\mathfrak{I}_{h}}}{\Gamma\left(\alpha_{h}j+1\right)\;{\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,_{h}}^{\alpha_{h}j-\mathfrak{I}_{h}}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{+\infty}{\frac{\overline{{\mbox{\large{\wedn{c}}}}\,}_{h}^{j}\,\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,_{\star,h}^{j}\,\alpha_{h}j(\alpha_{h}j-1)\dots(\alpha_{h}j-\mathfrak{I}_{h}+1)\,{\epsilon}^{\alpha_{h}j-\mathfrak{I}_{h}}}{\Gamma\left(\alpha_{h}j+1\right)\;{\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,_{h}}^{\alpha_{h}j-\mathfrak{I}_{h}}}}.

Accordingly, recalling (
5.30
), we find that

\begin{split}&\lim_{\epsilon\searrow 0}\epsilon^{\mathfrak{I}_{h}-\alpha_{h}}\partial^{\mathfrak{I}_{h}}_{t_{h}}\psi_{h}(0)=\lim_{\epsilon\searrow 0}\sum_{j=1}^{+\infty}{\frac{\overline{{\mbox{\large{\wedn{c}}}}\,}_{h}^{j}\,\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,_{\star,h}^{j}\,\alpha_{h}j(\alpha_{h}j-1)\dots(\alpha_{h}j-\mathfrak{I}_{h}+1)\,{\epsilon}^{\alpha_{h}(j-1)}}{\Gamma\left(\alpha_{h}j+1\right)\;{\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,_{h}}^{\alpha_{h}j-\mathfrak{I}_{h}}}}\\
&\qquad={\frac{\overline{{\mbox{\large{\wedn{c}}}}\,}_{h}\,\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,_{\star,h}\,\alpha_{h}(\alpha_{h}-1)\dots(\alpha_{h}-\mathfrak{I}_{h}+1)}{\Gamma\left(\alpha_{h}+1\right)\;\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,_{h}^{\alpha_{h}-\mathfrak{I}_{h}}}}={\frac{\overline{{\mbox{\large{\wedn{c}}}}\,}_{h}\,\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,_{h}^{\mathfrak{I}_{h}}\,\alpha_{h}(\alpha_{h}-1)\dots(\alpha_{h}-\mathfrak{I}_{h}+1)}{\Gamma\left(\alpha_{h}+1\right)}}.\end{split}

(5.95)

Also, recalling (
5.13
), we can write (
5.94
) as

0=\sum_{{|i|+|I|+|\mathfrak{I}|\leq K}\atop{|I|\leq|\overline{I}|}}\theta_{i,I,\mathfrak{I}}\,\partial^{i}_{x}\tau(0)\partial^{I_{1}}_{y_{1}}{\phi}_{1}(e_{1}+\epsilon Y_{1})\ldots\partial^{I_{M}}_{y_{M}}{\phi}_{M}(e_{M}+\epsilon Y_{M})\partial^{\mathfrak{I}_{1}}_{t_{1}}\psi_{1}(0)\ldots\partial^{\mathfrak{I}_{l}}_{t_{l}}\psi_{l}(0).

(5.96)

Moreover, we define

\Xi:=\left|\overline{I}\right|-\sum_{j=1}^{M}{s_{j}}+|\mathfrak{I}|-\sum_{h=1}^{l}{\alpha_{h}}.

Then, we
multiply (
5.96
) by
\epsilon^{\Xi}\in(0,+\infty)
, and we
send
\epsilon
to zero. In this way, we obtain from (
5.54
),
(
5.91
), (
5.95
) and (
5.96
) that

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon\searrow 0}\epsilon^{\Xi}\sum_{{|i|+|I|+|\mathfrak{I}|\leq K}\atop{|I|\leq|\overline{I}|}}\theta_{i,I,\mathfrak{I}}\,\partial_{x}^{i}\tau(0)\partial_{y_{1}}^{I_{1}}\phi_{1}\left(e_{1}+\epsilon Y_{1}\right)\ldots\partial_{y_{M}}^{I_{M}}\phi_{M}\left(e_{M}+\epsilon Y_{M}\right)\partial^{\mathfrak{I}_{1}}_{t_{1}}\psi_{1}(0)\ldots\partial^{\mathfrak{I}_{l}}_{t_{l}}\psi_{l}(0)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon\searrow 0}\sum_{{|i|+|I|+|\mathfrak{I}|\leq K}\atop{|I|\leq|\overline{I}|}}\epsilon^{|\overline{I}|-|I|}\theta_{i,I,\mathfrak{I}}\,\partial_{x}^{i}\tau(0)\epsilon^{|I_{1}|-s_{1}}\partial_{y_{1}}^{I_{1}}\phi_{1}\left(e_{1}+\epsilon Y_{1}\right)\ldots\epsilon^{|I_{M}|-s_{M}}\partial_{y_{M}}^{I_{M}}\phi_{M}\left(e_{M}+\epsilon Y_{M}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\epsilon^{\mathfrak{I}_{1}-\alpha_{1}}\partial^{\mathfrak{I}_{1}}_{t_{1}}\psi_{1}(0)\ldots\epsilon^{\mathfrak{I}_{l}-\alpha_{l}}\partial^{\mathfrak{I}_{l}}_{t_{l}}\psi_{l}(0)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{{|i|+|I|+|\mathfrak{I}|\leq K}\atop{|I|=|\overline{I}|}}C_{i,I,\mathfrak{I}}\,\theta_{i,I,\mathfrak{I}}\,\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{x}}}}\,_{1}^{i_{1}}\ldots\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{x}}}}\,_{n}^{i_{n}}\,e_{1}^{I_{1}}\ldots e_{M}^{I_{M}}\,\left(-e_{1}\cdot Y_{1}\right)_{+}^{s_{1}-|I_{1}|}\ldots\left(-e_{M}\cdot Y_{M}\right)_{+}^{s_{M}-|I_{M}|}\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,_{1}^{\mathfrak{I}_{1}}\ldots\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,_{l}^{\mathfrak{I}_{l}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(-e_{1}\cdot Y_{1}\right)_{+}^{s_{1}}\ldots\left(-e_{M}\cdot Y_{M}\right)_{+}^{s_{M}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times\sum_{{|i|+|I|+|\mathfrak{I}|\leq K}\atop{|I|=|\overline{I}|}}C_{i,I,\mathfrak{I}}\,\theta_{i,I,\mathfrak{I}}\;\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{x}}}}\,^{i}\,e^{I}\,\left(-e_{1}\cdot Y_{1}\right)_{+}^{-|I_{1}|}\ldots\left(-e_{M}\cdot Y_{M}\right)_{+}^{-|I_{M}|}\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,^{\mathfrak{I}},

for a suitable
C_{i,I,\mathfrak{I}}\neq 0
.

We observe that the latter equality
is valid for any choice of the free parameters
(\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{x}}}}\,,Y,\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,)

in an open subset of
\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}+\ldots+p_{n}}\times\mathbb{R}^{m_{1}+\ldots+m_{M}}\times\mathbb{R}^{l}
,
as prescribed in (
5.46
), (
5.81
) and (
5.89
).

Now, we take new free parameters
\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{y}}}}\,_{j}
with
\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{y}}}}\,_{j}\in\mathbb{R}^{m_{j}}\setminus\{0\}
for any
j=1,\ldots,M
, and perform in the latter identity the same change of variables done in (
5.58
), obtaining that

0=\sum_{{|i|+|I|+|\mathfrak{I}|\leq K}\atop{|I|=|\overline{I}|}}C_{i,I,\mathfrak{I}}\,\theta_{i,I,\mathfrak{I}}\;\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{x}}}}\,^{i}\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{y}}}}\,^{I}\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,^{\mathfrak{I}},

for some
C_{i,I,\mathfrak{I}}\neq 0
.
This completes the proof of (
5.15
) in case (
5.75
) is satisfied.

Hence, we now focus on the case in which (
5.76
) holds true.
For any
j\in\{1,\ldots,M\}
,
we consider the function
\psi\in H^{s_{j}}(\mathbb{R}^{m_{j}})\cap C^{s_{j}}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{m_{j}})

constructed in Lemma
4.15
and we call such function
\phi_{j}
,
to make it explicit its dependence on
j
in this case.
We recall that

(-\Delta)^{s_{j}}_{y_{j}}\phi_{j}(y_{j})=0\quad\text{ in }\,B_{1}^{m_{j}}.

(5.97)

Also, for every
j\in\{1,\ldots,M\}
, we let
e_{j}

and
Y_{j}
be as in (
5.45
) and (
5.46
).
Thanks to Lemma
4.15
and Remark
4.16
,
for any
I_{j}\in\mathbb{N}^{m_{j}}
, we know that

\lim_{\epsilon\searrow 0}\epsilon^{|I_{j}|-s_{j}}\partial_{y_{j}}^{I_{j}}\phi_{j}(e_{j}+\epsilon Y_{j})=\kappa_{s_{j}}e_{j}^{I_{j}}(-e_{j}\cdot Y_{j})_{+}^{s_{j}-|I_{j}|},

(5.98)

for some
\kappa_{s_{j}}\neq 0
.

Moreover, for any
h=1,\ldots,l
, we define
\overline{\tau}_{h}(t_{h})
as

\overline{\tau}_{h}(t_{h}):=\begin{cases}e^{\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,_{h}t_{h}}\quad&\text{if}\quad t_{h}\in[-1,+\infty),\\
\displaystyle e^{-\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,_{h}}\sum_{i=0}^{k_{h}-1}\frac{\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,_{h}^{i}}{i!}(t_{h}+1)^{i}\quad&\text{if}\quad t_{h}\in(-\infty,-1),\end{cases}

(5.99)

where

\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,=(\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,_{1},\ldots,\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,_{l})\in(1,2)^{l}
are free parameters.

We notice that, for any
h\in\{1,\ldots,l\}
and
\mathfrak{I}_{h}\in\mathbb{N}
,

\partial^{\mathfrak{I}_{h}}_{t_{h}}\overline{\tau}_{h}(0)=\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,_{h}^{\mathfrak{I}_{h}}.

(5.100)

Now, we
define

w(x,y,t):=\tau(x)\phi_{1}(y_{1}+e_{1}+\epsilon Y_{1})\ldots\phi_{M}(y_{M}+e_{M}+\epsilon Y_{M})\overline{\tau}_{1}(t_{1})\ldots\overline{\tau}_{l}(t_{l}),

(5.101)

where the setting of (
5.27
), (
5.90
) and (
5.99
) has been exploited.
We have that
w\in\mathcal{A}
. Moreover, we point out that, since
\tau
,
\phi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{M}
are
compactly supported, we have that
w\in C(\mathbb{R}^{N})\cap C_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{N-l})
, and, using Proposition
4.18
, for any
j\in\{1,\ldots,M\}
, it holds that
\phi_{j}\in C^{\infty}(\mathcal{N}_{j})
for some neighborhood
\mathcal{N}_{j}

of the origin in
\mathbb{R}^{m_{j}}
.
Hence
w\in C^{\infty}(\mathcal{N})
.

Furthermore,
using (
5.97
), when
y

is in a neighborhood of the origin we have that

\begin{split}\Lambda_{-\infty}w(x,y,t)&=\tau(x)\left({\mbox{\large{\wedn{b}}}}_{1}(-\Delta)^{s_{1}}_{y_{1}}\phi_{1}(y_{1}+e_{1}+\epsilon Y_{1})\right)\ldots\phi_{M}(y_{M}+e_{M}+\epsilon Y_{M})\overline{\tau}_{1}(t_{1})\ldots\overline{\tau}_{l}(t_{l})\\
&+\ldots+\tau(x)\phi_{1}(y_{1})\ldots\left({\mbox{\large{\wedn{b}}}}_{M}(-\Delta)^{s_{M}}_{Y_{M}}\phi_{M}(y_{M}+e_{M}+\epsilon Y_{M})\right)\overline{\tau}_{1}(t_{1})\ldots\overline{\tau}_{l}(t_{l})=0,\end{split}

which gives that
w\in\mathcal{H}
.

In addition, using (
5.13
), (
5.91
) and (
5.100
), we have that

\displaystyle 0=\theta\cdot\partial^{K}w(0)=\sum_{|\iota|\leq K}\theta_{i,I,\mathfrak{I}}\partial_{x}^{i}\partial_{y}^{I}\partial_{t}^{\mathfrak{I}}w(0)=\sum_{{|\iota|\leq K}\atop{|I|\leq|\overline{I}|}}\theta_{i,I,\mathfrak{I}}\partial_{x}^{i}\partial_{y}^{I}\partial_{t}^{\mathfrak{I}}w(0)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=\sum_{{|\iota|\leq K}\atop{|I|\leq|\overline{I}|}}\theta_{i,I,\mathfrak{I}}\,\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{x}}}}\,^{i}\partial_{y_{1}}^{I_{1}}\phi_{1}(e_{1}+\epsilon Y_{1})\ldots\partial_{y_{M}}^{I_{M}}\phi_{M}(e_{M}+\epsilon Y_{M})\,\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,^{\mathfrak{I}}.

Hence, we set

\Xi:=|\overline{I}|-\sum_{j=1}^{M}s_{j},

we multiply the latter identity by
\epsilon^{\Xi}

and we exploit (
5.98
). In this way, we find that

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon\searrow 0}\sum_{{|\iota|\leq K}\atop{|I|\leq|\overline{I}|}}\epsilon^{|\overline{I}|-|I|}\theta_{i,I,\mathfrak{I}}\,\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{x}}}}\,^{i}\,\epsilon^{|I_{1}|-s_{1}}\partial_{y_{1}}^{I_{1}}\phi_{1}(e_{1}+\epsilon Y_{1})\ldots\epsilon^{|I_{M}|-s_{M}}\partial_{y_{M}}^{I_{M}}\phi_{M}(e_{M}+\epsilon Y_{M})\,\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,^{\mathfrak{I}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{{|\iota|\leq K}\atop{|I|=|\overline{I}|}}\theta_{i,I,\mathfrak{I}}\,\kappa_{s_{j}}\,\,\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{x}}}}\,^{i}\,e^{I}\,(-e_{1}\cdot Y_{1})_{+}^{s_{1}-|I_{1}|}\ldots(-e_{M}\cdot Y_{M})_{+}^{s_{M}-|I_{M}|}\,\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,^{\mathfrak{I}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-e_{1}\cdot Y_{1})_{+}^{s_{1}}\ldots(-e_{M}\cdot Y_{M})_{+}^{s_{M}}\sum_{{|\iota|\leq K}\atop{|I|=|\overline{I}|}}\theta_{i,I,\mathfrak{I}}\,\kappa_{s_{j}}\,\,\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{x}}}}\,^{i}\,e^{I}\,(-e_{1}\cdot Y_{1})_{+}^{-|I_{1}|}\ldots(-e_{M}\cdot Y_{M})_{+}^{-|I_{M}|}\,\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,^{\mathfrak{I}},

and consequently

0=\sum_{{|\iota|\leq K}\atop{|I|=|\overline{I}|}}\theta_{i,I,\mathfrak{I}}\,\kappa_{s_{j}}\,\,\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{x}}}}\,^{i}\,e^{I}\,(-e_{1}\cdot Y_{1})_{+}^{-|I_{1}|}\ldots(-e_{M}\cdot Y_{M})_{+}^{-|I_{M}|}\,\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,^{\mathfrak{I}}.

(5.102)

Now we take
free parameters
\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{y}}}}\,\in\mathbb{R}^{m_{1}+\ldots+m_{M}}\setminus\{0\}

and we perform the same change of variables in (
5.58
).
In this way, we deduce from (
5.102
) that

\displaystyle 0=\sum_{{|i|+|I|+|\mathfrak{I}|\leq K}\atop{|I|=|\overline{I}|}}C_{i,I,\mathfrak{I}}\theta_{i,I,\mathfrak{I}}\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{x}}}}\,^{i}\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{y}}}}\,^{I}\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,^{\mathfrak{I}},

for some
C_{i,I,\mathfrak{I}}\neq 0
, and the first claim in (
5.15
) is proved
in this case as well.
∎

Proof of (
5.15
), case
4
.

Notice that if there exists
j\in\{1,\ldots,M\}
such that
{\mbox{\large{\wedn{b}}}}_{j}\neq 0
, we are in the setting of case
3
.
Therefore, we assume that
{\mbox{\large{\wedn{b}}}}_{j}=0
for every
j\in\{1,\ldots,M\}
.

We let
\psi

be the function constructed in Lemma
3.3
.
For each
h\in\{1,\dots,l\}
,
we let
\overline{\psi}_{h}(t_{h}):=\psi(t_{h})
,
to make the dependence on
h
clear and explicit.
Then, by formulas (
3.8
) and (
3.9
),
we know that

D^{\alpha_{h}}_{t_{h},0}\overline{\psi}_{h}(t_{h})=0\quad\text{ in }\,(1,+\infty)

(5.103)

and, for every
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
,

\lim_{\epsilon\searrow 0}\epsilon^{\ell-\alpha_{h}}\partial^{\ell}_{t_{h}}\overline{\psi}_{h}(1+\epsilon t_{h})=\kappa_{h,\ell}\;t_{h}^{\alpha_{h}-\ell},

(5.104)

in the sense of distribution,
for some
\kappa_{h,\ell}\neq 0
.

Now,
we introduce a set of auxiliary parameters
\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,=(\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,_{1},\ldots,\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,_{l})\in(1,2)^{l}
, and fix
\epsilon
sufficiently small
possibly depending on the parameters. Then, we define

a=(a_{1},\dots,a_{l}):=\left(-\frac{\epsilon}{\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,_{1}}-1,\ldots,-\frac{\epsilon}{\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,_{l}}-1\right)\in(-2,0)^{l},

(5.105)

and

\psi_{h}(t_{h}):=\overline{\psi}_{h}(t_{h}-a_{h}).

(5.106)

With a simple computation we have that the function
in (
5.106
) satisfies

D^{\alpha_{h}}_{t_{h},a_{h}}\psi_{h}(t_{h})=D^{\alpha_{h}}_{t_{h},0}\overline{\psi}_{h}(t_{h}-a_{h})=0\quad\text{in }\,(1+a_{h},+\infty)=\left(-\frac{\epsilon}{\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,_{h}},+\infty\right),

(5.107)

thanks to (
5.103
).
In addition, for every
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
, we have that
\partial^{\ell}_{t_{h}}\psi_{h}(t_{h})=\partial^{\ell}_{t_{h}}\overline{\psi}_{h}(t_{h}-a_{h})
, and therefore,
in light of (
5.104
) and (
5.105
),

\epsilon^{\ell-\alpha_{h}}\partial^{\ell}_{t_{h}}\psi_{h}(0)=\epsilon^{\ell-\alpha_{h}}\partial^{\ell}_{t_{h}}\overline{\psi}_{h}(-a_{h})=\epsilon^{\ell-\alpha_{h}}\partial^{\ell}_{t_{h}}\overline{\psi}_{h}\left(1+\frac{\epsilon}{\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,_{h}}\right)\to\kappa_{h,\ell}\;\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,_{h}^{\ell-\alpha_{h}},

(5.108)

in the sense of distributions, as
\epsilon\searrow 0
.

Moreover, since for any
h=1,\ldots,l
,
\psi_{h}\in C^{k_{h},\alpha_{h}}_{a_{h}}
, we can consider the extension

\psi^{\star}_{h}(t_{h}):=\begin{cases}\psi_{h}(t_{h})&\quad\text{ if }\,t_{h}\in[a_{h},+\infty),\\
\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{k_{h}-1}\frac{\psi_{h}^{(i)}(a_{h})}{i!}(t_{h}-a_{h})^{i}&\quad\text{ if }\,t_{h}\in(-\infty,a_{h}),\end{cases}

(5.109)

and, using Lemma A.3 in
[
CDV18
]
with
u:=\psi_{h}
,
a:=-\infty
,
b:=a_{h}
and
u_{\star}:=\psi^{\star}_{h}
, we have that

\psi^{\star}_{h}\in C^{k_{h},\alpha_{h}}_{-\infty}\quad\text{and}\quad D^{\alpha_{h}}_{t_{h},-\infty}\psi_{h}^{\star}=D^{\alpha_{h}}_{t_{h},a_{h}}\psi_{h}=0\quad\text{in every interval }\,I\Subset\left(-\frac{\epsilon}{\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,_{h}},+\infty\right).

(5.110)

Now, we fix a set of free parameters
\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{y}}}}\,=\left(\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{y}}}}\,_{1},\ldots,\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{y}}}}\,_{M}\right)\in\mathbb{R}^{m_{1}+\ldots+m_{M}}
, and consider
\overline{\tau}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{m_{1}+\ldots+m_{M}})
, such that

\overline{\tau}(y):=\begin{cases}\exp\left({\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{y}}}}\,\cdot y}\right)\quad&\text{if }\quad y\in B_{1}^{m_{1}+\ldots+m_{M}},\\
0\quad&\text{if }\quad y\in\mathbb{R}^{m_{1}+\ldots+m_{M}}\setminus B_{2}^{m_{1}+\ldots+m_{M}},\end{cases}

(5.111)

where

\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{y}}}}\,\cdot y=\sum_{j=1}^{M}\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{y}}}}\,_{j}\cdot y_{j},

denotes the standard scalar product.

We notice that, for any multi-index
I\in\mathbb{N}^{m_{1}+\ldots m_{M}}
,

\partial^{I}_{y}\overline{\tau}(0)=\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{y}}}}\,^{I},

(5.112)

where the multi-index notation has been used.

Now, we define

w(x,y,t):=\tau(x)\overline{\tau}(y)\psi^{\star}_{1}(t_{1})\ldots\psi^{\star}_{l}(t_{l}),

(5.113)

where the setting in (
5.90
),
(
5.109
) and (
5.111
) has been exploited.

Using (
5.110
), we have that, for any
(x,y)

in a neighborhood of the origin and
t\in\left(-\frac{\epsilon}{2},+\infty\right)^{l}
,

\begin{split}\Lambda_{-\infty}w(x,y,t)&=\tau(x)\overline{\tau}(y)\left({\mbox{\large{\wedn{c}}}}\,_{1}D^{\alpha_{1}}_{t_{1},-\infty}\psi^{\star}_{1}(t_{1})\right)\ldots\psi^{\star}_{l}(t_{l})\\
&+\ldots+\tau(x)\overline{\tau}(y)\psi^{\star}_{1}(t_{1})\ldots\left({\mbox{\large{\wedn{c}}}}\,_{l}D^{\alpha_{l}}_{t_{l},-\infty}\psi^{\star}_{l}(t_{l})\right)=0.\end{split}

We have that
w\in\mathcal{A}
, and, since
\tau
and
\overline{\tau}
are compactly supported, we
also have that
w\in C(\mathbb{R}^{N})\cap C_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{N-l})
.
Also, from Lemma
3.3
, for any
h\in\{1,\ldots,l\}
, we know that
\overline{\psi}_{h}\in C^{\infty}((1,+\infty))
, hence
\psi_{h}\in C^{\infty}\left(\left(-\frac{\epsilon}{\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,_{h}},+\infty\right)\right)
.
Thus,
w\in C^{\infty}(\mathcal{N})
, and consequently
w\in\mathcal{H}
.

Recalling (
5.14
), (
5.91
), and (
5.112
), we have that

\begin{split}&0=\theta\cdot\partial^{K}w(0)=\sum_{|\iota|\leq K}\theta_{i,I,\mathfrak{I}}\partial_{x}^{i}\partial_{y}^{I}\partial_{t}^{\mathfrak{I}}w(0)=\sum_{{|\iota|\leq K}\atop{|\mathfrak{I}|\leq|\overline{\mathfrak{I}}|}}\theta_{i,I,\mathfrak{I}}\partial_{x}^{i}\partial_{y}^{I}\partial_{t}^{\mathfrak{I}}w(0)\\
&\qquad\qquad=\sum_{{|\iota|\leq K}\atop{|\mathfrak{I}|\leq|\overline{\mathfrak{I}}|}}\theta_{i,I,\mathfrak{I}}\,\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{x}}}}\,^{i}\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{y}}}}\,^{I}\partial_{t_{1}}^{\mathfrak{I}_{1}}\psi_{1}(0)\ldots\partial_{t_{l}}^{\mathfrak{I}_{l}}\psi_{l}(0).\end{split}

(5.114)

Hence, we set

\Xi:=|\overline{\mathfrak{I}}|-\sum_{h=1}^{l}\alpha_{h},

we multiply the identity in (
5.114
) by
\epsilon^{\Xi}

and we exploit (
5.108
). In this way, we find that

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon\searrow 0}\sum_{{|\iota|\leq K}\atop{|\mathfrak{I}|\leq|\overline{\mathfrak{I}}|}}\epsilon^{|\overline{\mathfrak{I}}|-|\mathfrak{I}|}\theta_{i,I,\mathfrak{I}}\,\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{x}}}}\,^{i}\,\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{y}}}}\,^{I}\,\epsilon^{\mathfrak{I}_{1}-\alpha_{1}}\partial_{t_{1}}^{\mathfrak{I}_{1}}{\psi}_{1}(0)\ldots\epsilon^{\mathfrak{I}_{l}-\alpha_{l}}\partial_{t_{l}}^{\mathfrak{I}_{l}}{\psi}_{l}(0)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{{|\iota|\leq K}\atop{|\mathfrak{I}|=|\overline{\mathfrak{I}}|}}\theta_{i,I,\mathfrak{I}}\;\kappa_{1,\mathfrak{I}_{1}}\dots\kappa_{l,\mathfrak{I}_{l}}\;\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{x}}}}\,^{i}\,\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{y}}}}\,^{I}\,\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,_{1}^{\mathfrak{I}_{1}-\alpha_{1}}\ldots\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,_{l}^{\mathfrak{I}_{l}-\alpha_{l}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,_{1}^{-\alpha_{1}}\ldots\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,_{l}^{-\alpha_{l}}\sum_{{|\iota|\leq K}\atop{|\mathfrak{I}|=|\overline{\mathfrak{I}}|}}\theta_{i,I,\mathfrak{I}}\;\kappa_{1,\mathfrak{I}_{1}}\dots\kappa_{l,\mathfrak{I}_{l}}\;\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{x}}}}\,^{i}\,\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{y}}}}\,^{I}\,\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,_{1}^{\mathfrak{I}_{1}}\ldots\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,_{l}^{\mathfrak{I}_{l}},

and consequently

0=\sum_{{|\iota|\leq K}\atop{|\mathfrak{I}|=|\overline{\mathfrak{I}}|}}\theta_{i,I,\mathfrak{I}}\;\kappa_{1,\mathfrak{I}_{1}}\dots\kappa_{l,\mathfrak{I}_{l}}\;\,\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{x}}}}\,^{i}\,\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{y}}}}\,^{I}\,\underline{\mbox{\large{\wedn{t}}}}\,^{\mathfrak{I}},

and the second claim in (
5.15
) is proved
in this case as well.
∎

5.3
Every function is locally
\Lambda_{-\infty}
-harmonic up to a small error,
and completion of the proof of Theorem
5.1

In this section we complete the proof of Theorem
5.1

(which in turn implies Theorem
2.1
via Lemma
5.2
).
By standard approximation arguments we can reduce to
the case in which
f
is a polynomial, and hence, by the linearity
of the operator
\Lambda_{-\infty}
, to the case in which is a monomial.
The details of the proof are therefore the following:

5.3.1
Proof of Theorem
5.1
when
f
is a monomial

We prove Theorem
5.1
under the initial assumption
that
f
is a monomial, that is

f\left(x,y,t\right)=\frac{x_{1}^{i_{1}}\ldots x_{n}^{i_{n}}y_{1}^{I_{1}}\ldots y_{M}^{I_{M}}t_{1}^{\mathfrak{I}_{1}}\ldots t_{l}^{\mathfrak{I}_{l}}}{\iota!}=\frac{x^{i}y^{I}t^{\mathfrak{I}}}{\iota!}=\frac{(x,y,t)^{\iota}}{\iota!},

(5.115)

where
\iota!:=i_{1}!\ldots i_{n}!I_{1}!\ldots I_{M}!\mathfrak{I}_{1}!\ldots\mathfrak{I}_{l}!
and
I_{\beta}!:=I_{\beta,1}!\ldots I_{\beta,m_{\beta}}!
,
i_{\chi}!:=i_{\chi,1}!\ldots i_{\chi,p_{\chi}}!
for all
\beta=1,\ldots M
. and
\chi=1,\ldots,n
.
To this end, we argue as follows.
We
consider
\eta\in\left(0,1\right)
, to be taken
sufficiently small with respect to the
parameter
\epsilon>0
which has been fixed
in the statement of Theorem
5.1
, and we define

{\mathcal{T}}_{\eta}(x,y,t):=\left(\eta^{\frac{1}{r_{1}}}x_{1},\ldots,\eta^{\frac{1}{r_{n}}}x_{n},\eta^{\frac{1}{2s_{1}}}y_{1},\ldots,\eta^{\frac{1}{2s_{M}}}y_{M},\eta^{\frac{1}{\alpha_{1}}}t_{1},\ldots,\eta^{\frac{1}{\alpha_{l}}}t_{l}\right).

We also define

\gamma:=\sum_{j=1}^{n}{\frac{|i_{j}|}{r_{j}}}+\sum_{j=1}^{M}{\frac{\left|I_{j}\right|}{2s_{j}}}+\sum_{j=1}^{l}{\frac{\mathfrak{I}_{j}}{\alpha_{j}}},

(5.116)

and

\delta:=\min\left\{\frac{1}{r_{1}},\ldots,\frac{1}{r_{n}},\frac{1}{2s_{1}},\ldots,\frac{1}{2s_{M}},\frac{1}{\alpha_{1}},\ldots,\frac{1}{\alpha_{l}}\right\}.

(5.117)

We also take
K_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
such that

K_{0}\geq\frac{\gamma+1}{\delta}

(5.118)

and we let

K:=K_{0}+\left|i\right|+\left|I\right|+\left|\mathfrak{I}\right|+\ell=K_{0}+\left|\iota\right|+\ell,

(5.119)

where
\ell
is the fixed integer given in the statement of Theorem
5.1
.

By Lemma
5.3
, there exist a neighborhood
\mathcal{N}

of the origin and a function
w\in C\left(\mathbb{R}^{N}\right)\cap C_{0}\left(\mathbb{R}^{N-l}\right)\cap C^{\infty}\left(\mathcal{N}\right)\cap\mathcal{A}
such that

\Lambda_{-\infty}w=0
in
\mathcal{N}
,

(5.120)

and such that

\begin{split}&{\mbox{all the derivatives of $w$ in 0 up to order $K$ vanish,}}\\
&{\mbox{with the exception of $\partial^{\iota}w\left(0\right)$ which equals\penalty\ $1$,}}\end{split}

(5.121)

being
\iota
as in (
5.115
).
Recalling the definition of
\mathcal{A}
on page
5.2
,
we also know that

\partial^{k_{h}}_{t_{h}}w=0

in
(-\infty,a_{h})
,

(5.122)

for suitable
a_{h}\in(-2,0)
,
for all
h\in\{1,\dots,l\}
.

In this way, setting

g:=w-f,

(5.123)

we deduce from (
5.121
) that

\partial^{\sigma}g\left(0\right)=0\quad\text{ for any }\sigma\in\mathbb{N}^{N}\text{ with }\left|\sigma\right|\leq K.

Accordingly, in
\mathcal{N}
we can write

g\left(x,y,t\right)=\sum_{\left|\tau\right|\geq K+1}{x^{\tau_{1}}y^{\tau_{2}}t^{\tau_{3}}h_{\tau}\left(x,y,t\right)},

(5.124)

for some
h_{\tau}
smooth in
\mathcal{N}
, where the multi-index
notation
\tau=(\tau_{1},\tau_{2},\tau_{3})
has been used.

Now, we define

u\left(x,y,t\right):=\frac{1}{\eta^{\gamma}}w\left({\mathcal{T}}_{\eta}(x,y,t)\right).

(5.125)

In light of (
5.122
), we notice that

\partial^{k_{h}}_{t_{h}}u=0

in
(-\infty,a_{h}/\eta^{\frac{1}{\alpha_{h}}})
, for all
h\in\{1,\dots,l\}
,
and therefore
u\in C\left(\mathbb{R}^{N}\right)\cap C_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{N-l})\cap C^{\infty}\left({\mathcal{T}}_{\eta}(\mathcal{N})\right)\cap\mathcal{A}
. We also claim that

{\mathcal{T}}_{\eta}([-1,1]^{N-l}\times(a_{1},+\infty)\times\ldots\times(a_{l},+\infty))\subseteq{\mathcal{N}}.

(5.126)

To check this, let
(x,y,t)\in[-1,1]^{N-l}\times(a_{1}+\infty)\times\ldots\times(a_{l},+\infty)

and
(X,Y,T):={\mathcal{T}}_{\eta}(x,y,t)
.
Then, we have that
|X_{1}|=\eta^{\frac{1}{r_{1}}}|x_{1}|\leq\eta^{\frac{1}{r_{1}}}
,

|Y_{1}|=\eta^{\frac{1}{2s_{1}}}|y_{1}|\leq\eta^{\frac{1}{2s_{1}}}
,

T_{1}=\eta^{\frac{1}{\alpha_{1}}}t_{1}>a_{1}\eta^{\frac{1}{\alpha_{1}}}>-1
,
provided
\eta
is small enough.
Repeating this argument, we obtain that, for small
\eta
,

(X,Y,T)
is as
close to the origin as we wish.

(5.127)

From (
5.127
) and the fact that
{\mathcal{N}}

is an open set, we infer that
(X,Y,T)\in{\mathcal{N}}
,
and this proves (
5.126
).

Thanks to (
5.120
) and (
5.126
), we have that, in
B_{1}^{N-l}\times(-1,+\infty)^{l}
,

\displaystyle\eta^{\gamma-1}\,\Lambda_{-\infty}u\left(x,y,t\right)

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{n}{{\mbox{\large{\wedn{a}}}}_{j}\partial_{x_{j}}^{r_{j}}w\left({\mathcal{T}}_{\eta}(x,y,t)\right)}+\sum_{j=1}^{M}{{\mbox{\large{\wedn{b}}}}_{j}(-\Delta)^{s_{j}}_{y_{j}}w\left({\mathcal{T}}_{\eta}(x,y,t)\right)}+\sum_{j=1}^{l}{{\mbox{\large{\wedn{c}}}}\,_{j}D_{t_{h},-\infty}^{\alpha_{h}}w\left({\mathcal{T}}_{\eta}(x,y,t)\right)}

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\Lambda_{-\infty}w\left({\mathcal{T}}_{\eta}(x,y,t)\right)

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle 0.

These observations establish that
u
solves the equation in
B_{1}^{N-l}\times(-1+\infty)^{l}
and
u
vanishes when
|(x,y)|\geq R
,
for some
R>1
, and thus the claims in (
5.3
)
and (
5.4
)
are proved.

Now we prove that
u
approximates
f
, as claimed in (
5.5
). For this, using the monomial structure of
f
in (
5.115
)
and the definition of
\gamma
in (
5.116
), we have,
in a multi-index notation,

\begin{split}&\frac{1}{\eta^{\gamma}}f\left({\mathcal{T}}_{\eta}(x,y,t)\right)=\frac{1}{\eta^{\gamma}\,\iota!}\;(\eta^{\frac{1}{r}}x)^{i}(\eta^{\frac{1}{2s}}y)^{I}\big(\eta^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}t\big)^{\mathfrak{I}}=\frac{1}{\iota!}x^{i}y^{I}t^{\mathfrak{I}}=f(x,y,t).\end{split}

(5.128)

Consequently, by (
5.123
), (
5.124
), (
5.125
) and (
5.128
),

\displaystyle u\left(x,y,t\right)-f\left(x,y,t\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\eta^{\gamma}}g\left(\eta^{\frac{1}{r_{1}}}x_{1},\ldots,\eta^{\frac{1}{r_{n}}}x_{n},\eta^{\frac{1}{2s_{1}}}y_{1},\ldots,\eta^{\frac{1}{2s_{M}}}y_{M},\eta^{\frac{1}{\alpha_{1}}}t_{1},\ldots,\eta^{\frac{1}{\alpha_{l}}}t_{l}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\left|\tau\right|\geq K+1}{\eta^{\left|\frac{\tau_{1}}{r}\right|+\left|\frac{\tau_{2}}{2s}\right|+\left|\frac{\tau_{3}}{\alpha}\right|-\gamma}x^{\tau_{1}}y^{\tau_{2}}t^{\tau_{3}}h_{\tau}\left(\eta^{\frac{1}{r}}x,\eta^{\frac{1}{2s}}y,\eta^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}t\right)},

where a multi-index notation has been used, e.g. we have written

\frac{\tau_{1}}{r}:=\left(\frac{\tau_{1,1}}{r_{1}},\dots,\frac{\tau_{1,n}}{r_{n}}\right)\in\mathbb{R}^{n}.

Therefore, for any multi-index
\beta=\left(\beta_{1},\beta_{2},\beta_{3}\right)
with
\left|\beta\right|\leq\ell
,

\begin{split}&\partial^{\beta}\left(u\left(x,y,t\right)-f\left(x,y,t\right)\right)\\
=\,&\partial^{\beta_{1}}_{x}\partial^{\beta_{2}}_{y}\partial^{\beta_{3}}_{t}\left(u\left(x,y,t\right)-f\left(x,y,t\right)\right)\\
=\,&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\left|\beta^{\prime}_{1}\right|+\left|\beta^{\prime\prime}_{1}\right|=\left|\beta_{1}\right|\\
\left|\beta^{\prime}_{2}\right|+\left|\beta^{\prime\prime}_{2}\right|=\left|\beta_{2}\right|\\
\left|\beta^{\prime}_{3}\right|+\left|\beta^{\prime\prime}_{3}\right|=\left|\beta_{3}\right|\\
\left|\tau\right|\geq K+1\end{subarray}}{c_{\tau,\beta}\;\eta^{\kappa_{\tau,\beta}}\;x^{\tau_{1}-\beta^{\prime}_{1}}y^{\tau_{2}-\beta^{\prime}_{2}}t^{\tau_{3}-\beta^{\prime}_{3}}\partial_{x}^{\beta^{\prime\prime}_{1}}\partial_{y}^{\beta^{\prime\prime}_{2}}\partial_{t}^{\beta^{\prime\prime}_{3}}h_{\tau}\left(\eta^{\frac{1}{r}}x,\eta^{\frac{1}{2s}}y,\eta^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}t\right)},\end{split}

(5.129)

where

\kappa_{\tau,\beta}:=\left|\frac{\tau_{1}}{r}\right|+\left|\frac{\tau_{2}}{2s}\right|+\left|\frac{\tau_{3}}{\alpha}\right|-\gamma+\left|\frac{\beta^{\prime\prime}_{1}}{r}\right|+\left|\frac{\beta^{\prime\prime}_{2}}{2s}\right|+\left|\frac{\beta^{\prime\prime}_{3}}{\alpha}\right|,

for suitable coefficients
c_{\tau,\beta}
. Thus, to complete the proof of (
5.5
), we need to show that this quantity is small if so is
\eta
.
To this aim, we use (
5.117
), (
5.118
)
and (
5.119
) to see that

\displaystyle\kappa_{\tau,\beta}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\tau_{1}}{r}\right|+\left|\frac{\tau_{2}}{2s}\right|+\left|\frac{\tau_{3}}{\alpha}\right|-\gamma

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\delta\left(\left|\tau_{1}\right|+\left|\tau_{2}\right|+\left|\tau_{3}\right|\right)-\gamma

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle K\delta-\gamma

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle K_{0}\delta-\gamma

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle 1.

Consequently, we deduce from (
5.129
) that
\left\|u-f\right\|_{C^{\ell}\left(B_{1}^{N}\right)}\leq C\eta
for some
C>0
. By choosing
\eta
sufficiently small with respect to
\epsilon
, this implies the claim in (
5.5
). This completes the proof of
Theorem
5.1
when
f
is a monomial.

5.3.2
Proof of Theorem
5.1
when
f
is a polynomial

Now, we consider the case in which
f
is a polynomial. In this case, we can write
f
as

f\left(x,y,t\right)=\sum_{j=1}^{J}c_{j}f_{j}\left(x,y,t\right),

where each
f_{j}
is a monomial,
J\in\mathbb{N}
and
c_{j}\in\mathbb{R}
for all
j=1,\ldots,J
.

Let

c:=\max_{j\in\{1,\dots,J\}}c_{j}.

Then,
by the work done in Subsection
5.3.1
,
we know that the claim
in Theorem
5.1
holds true for each
f_{j}
, and so we can find
a_{j}\in(-\infty,0)^{l}
,
u_{j}\in C^{\infty}\left(B_{1}^{N}\right)\cap C\left(\mathbb{R}^{N}\right)\cap\mathcal{A}

and
R_{j}>1
such that
\Lambda_{-\infty}u_{j}=0
in
B_{1}^{N-l}\times(-1,+\infty)^{l}
,
\left\|u_{j}-f_{j}\right\|_{C^{\ell}\left(B_{1}^{N}\right)}\leq\epsilon
and
u_{j}=0
if
|(x,y)|\geq R_{j}
.

Hence, we set

u\left(x,y,t\right):=\sum_{j=1}^{J}c_{j}u_{j}\left(x,y,t\right),

and we see that

\left\|u-f\right\|_{C^{\ell}\left(B_{1}^{N}\right)}\leq\sum_{j=1}^{J}{\left|c_{j}\right|\left\|u_{j}-f_{j}\right\|_{C^{\ell}\left(B_{1}^{N}\right)}}\leq cJ\epsilon.

(5.130)

Also,
\Lambda_{-\infty}u=0
thanks to the linearity of
\Lambda_{-\infty}
in
B_{1}^{N-l}\times(-1,+\infty)^{l}
. Finally,
u
is supported in
B_{R}^{N-l}
in the variables
(x,y)
, being

R:=\max_{j\in\{1,\dots,J\}}R_{j}.

This proves
Theorem
5.1
when
f
is a polynomial (up to replacing
\epsilon
with
cJ\epsilon
).

5.3.3
Proof of Theorem
5.1
for a general
f

Now we deal with the case of a general
f
. To this end, we exploit
Lemma 2 in
[
MR3626547
]
and we see that
there exists a polynomial
\tilde{f}
such that

\|f-\tilde{f}\|_{C^{\ell}(B_{1}^{N})}\leq\epsilon.

(5.131)

Then, applying the result already proven in Subsection
5.3.2

to the polynomial
\tilde{f}
,
we can find
a\in(-\infty,0)^{l}
,
u\in C^{\infty}\left(B_{1}^{N}\right)\cap C\left(\mathbb{R}^{N}\right)\cap\mathcal{A}
and
R>1
such that

\displaystyle\Lambda_{-\infty}u=0\quad{\mbox{ in }}B_{1}^{N-l}\times(-1,+\infty)^{l},

\displaystyle u=0\qquad\quad{\mbox{ if }}|(x,y)|\geq R,

\displaystyle\partial^{k_{h}}_{t_{h}}u=0\quad\quad{\mbox{if }}t_{h}\in(-\infty,a_{h}),\qquad{\mbox{ for all }}h\in\{1,\dots,l\},

and

\displaystyle\|u-\tilde{f}\|_{C^{\ell}(B_{1}^{N})}\leq\epsilon.

Then, recalling (
5.131
), we see that

\|u-f\|_{C^{\ell}(B_{1}^{N})}\leq\|u-\tilde{f}\|_{C^{\ell}(B_{1}^{N})}+\|f-\tilde{f}\|_{C^{\ell}(B_{1}^{N})}\leq 2\epsilon.

Hence, the proof
of Theorem
5.1
is complete.
∎

Appendix A
Some applications

In this appendix we give
some applications of the approximation results obtained and discussed
in this book. These examples exploit particular cases of the
operator
\Lambda_{a}
, namely, when
s\in(0,1)
and
\Lambda_{a}
is the fractional Laplacian
(-\Delta)^{s}
, or the fractional heat operator
\partial_{t}+(-\Delta)^{s}
.
Similar applications have been pointed
out in
[
MR3579567
,
AV
,
2017arXiv170806300R
]
.

Example A.1

(The classical Harnack inequality fails for
s
-harmonic functions)
.

Harnack inequality, in its classical formulation, says that if
u
is a nontrivial and nonnegative harmonic function in
B_{1}
then, for any
0<r<1
, there exists
0<c=c(n,r)
such that

\sup_{B_{r}}u\leq c\inf_{B_{r}}u.

(A.1)

The same result is not true for
s
-harmonic functions. To construct a counterexample, consider the smooth function
f(x)=|x|^{2}
, and, for a small
\epsilon>0
, let
v=v_{\epsilon}
be the function
provided by Theorem
2.1
, where we choose
\ell=0
.
Notice that, if
x\in B_{1}\setminus B_{r/2}
,

v(x)\geq f(x)-\left\|v-f\right\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{1})}\geq\frac{r^{2}}{4}-\epsilon>\frac{r^{2}}{8},

provided
\epsilon
is small enough, while

v(0)\leq f(0)+\left\|v-f\right\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{1})}\leq\epsilon<\frac{r^{2}}{8}.

Hence, we have that
v(0)<v(x)
for any
x\in B_{1}\setminus B_{r/2}
, and therefore the minimum of
v
in
B_{1}
is attained in some point
\overline{x}\in\overline{B_{r/2}}
. Then, we define

u(x):=v(x)-v(\overline{x}).

Notice that
u
is
s
-harmonic in
B_{1}
since so does
v
.
Also,
u\geq 0
in
B_{1}
by construction, and
u>0
in
B_{1}\setminus B_{r/2}
. On the other hand, since
\overline{x}\in B_{r}

\inf_{B_{r}}u=u(\overline{x})=0,

which implies that
u
cannot satisfies an inequality such as (
A.1
).

As a matter of fact, in the fractional case, the analogue of the Harnack inequality requires
u

to be nonnegative in the whole of
{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
, hence a “global” condition
is needed to obtain a “local” oscillation bound. See e.g.
[
MR2817382
]

and the references therein for a complete discussion
of nonlocal Harnack inequalities.

Example A.2

(A logistic equation with nonlocal interactions)
.

We consider the logistic equation taken into account in
[
MR3579567
]

-(-\Delta)^{s}u+(\sigma-\mu u)u+\tau(J*u)=0,

(A.2)

where
s\in(0,1]
,
\tau\in[0,+\infty)
and
\sigma,\mu,J
are nonnegative functions. The symbol
*
denotes as usual the convolution product between
J
and
u
. Moreover, the convolution kernel
J
is assumed to be of unit mass and even, namely

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}J(x)dx=1

and

J(-x)=J(x)\quad\text{for any}\,x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}.

In this framework, the solution
u
denotes the density of a population living in some environment
\Omega\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
, while the functions
\sigma
and
\mu
model respectively the growing and dying effects on the population.
The equation is driven by the fractional Laplacian that models a nonlocal dispersal strategy which has been observed experimentally in nature, and may be related to optimal hunting strategies and adaptation to the environment stimulated by natural selection.

We state here a result which translates the fact that a
population with a nonlocal strategy can plan the distribution of resources in a strategic region better than populations with a local one.

Namely, fixed
\Omega=B_{1}
, one can find a solution of a slightly perturbed version
of (
A.2
) in
B_{1}
, compactly supported in a larger ball
B_{R_{\epsilon}}
,
where
\epsilon\in(0,1)
denotes the perturbation.

The strategic plan consists in properly adjusting
the resources in
B_{R_{\epsilon}}\setminus B_{1}
(that is,
a bounded region in which the equation is not satisfied) in order to consume almost
all the given resources in
B_{1}
.

The detailed statement goes as follows:

Theorem A.3
.

Let
s\in(0,1)
and
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
,
\ell\geq 2
. Assume that

\sigma,\mu\in C^{\ell}(\overline{B_{2}})
, with

\inf_{\overline{B_{2}}}\mu>0,\qquad\inf_{\overline{B_{2}}}\sigma>0.

Fixed
\epsilon\in(0,1)
, there exist
a nonnegative function
u_{\epsilon}
,

R_{\epsilon}>2
and
\sigma_{\epsilon}\in C^{\ell}(\overline{B_{1}})
such that

(-\Delta)^{s}u_{\epsilon}=(\sigma_{\epsilon}-\mu u_{\epsilon})u_{\epsilon}+\tau(J*u_{\epsilon})\quad\mbox{ in }B_{1},

u_{\epsilon}=0\quad\text{in }\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus B_{R_{\epsilon}},

\left\|\sigma_{\epsilon}-\sigma\right\|_{C^{\ell}(\overline{B_{1}})}\leq\epsilon,

u_{\epsilon}\geq\mu^{-1}\sigma_{\epsilon}\quad\text{in }B_{1}.

Example A.4
.

Higher order nonlocal equations also appear naturally
in several contexts, see e.g.
[
MR3051400
]
for a nonlocal version
of the Cahn-Hilliard phase coexistence model.
Higher orders operators have also appeared in connection with logistic equations,
see e.g.
[
MR3578282
]
. In this spirit, we point out a version
of Theorem
A.3
which is new and relies
on Theorem
2.1
. Its content is that nonlocal logistic equations
(of any order and with nonlocality given in either time or space, or both)
admits solutions which can arbitrarily well adapt to any given resource.
The precise statement is the following:

Theorem A.5
.

Let
s\in(0,+\infty)
,
\alpha\in(0,+\infty)
and
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
,
\ell\geq 2
.
Assume that

either
s\not\in{\mathbb{N}}
or
\alpha\not\in{\mathbb{N}}
.

(A.3)

Let
\sigma,\mu\in C^{\ell}(\overline{B_{2}})
, with

\inf_{\overline{B_{1}}}\mu>0.

(A.4)

Fixed
\epsilon\in(0,1)
, there exist
a nonnegative function
u_{\epsilon}
,

R_{\epsilon}>2
,
a_{\epsilon}<0
,
and
\sigma_{\epsilon}\in C^{\ell}(\overline{B_{1}})
such that

\begin{split}&D^{\alpha}_{t,a_{\epsilon}}u_{\epsilon}(x,t)+(-\Delta)^{s}u_{\epsilon}(x,t)=\Big(\sigma_{\epsilon}(x,t)-\mu(x,t)u_{\epsilon}(x,t)\Big)\,u_{\epsilon}(x,t)\\
&\qquad\mbox{ for all $(x,t)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p}\times{\mathbb{R}}$ with $|(x,t)|<1$},\end{split}

(A.5)

u_{\epsilon}(x,t)=0\quad\text{if }|(x,t)|\geq R_{\epsilon},

(A.6)

\left\|\sigma_{\epsilon}-\sigma\right\|_{C^{\ell}(\overline{B_{1}})}\leq\epsilon,

(A.7)

u_{\epsilon}=\mu^{-1}\sigma_{\epsilon}\geq\mu^{-1}\sigma-\epsilon\quad\text{in }B_{1}.

(A.8)

Proof.

We use Theorem
2.1
in the case in which
\Lambda_{a}:=D^{\alpha}_{t,a}+(-\Delta)^{s}
. Let
f:=\sigma/\mu
. Then,
by Theorem
2.1
, which can be exploited here in view of (
A.3
),
we obtain the existence of suitable
u_{\epsilon}
,

R_{\epsilon}>2
and
a_{\epsilon}<0
satisfying (
A.6
),

\begin{split}&D^{\alpha}_{t,a_{\epsilon}}u_{\epsilon}(x,t)+(-\Delta)^{s}u_{\epsilon}(x,t)=0\\
&\qquad\mbox{ for all $(x,t)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p}\times{\mathbb{R}}$ with $|(x,t)|<1$},\end{split}

(A.9)

and

\left\|u_{\epsilon}-f\right\|_{C^{\ell}(\overline{B_{1}})}\leq\epsilon.

(A.10)

Then, we set
\sigma_{\epsilon}:=\mu u_{\epsilon}
, and then, by (
A.10
),

\begin{split}\left\|\sigma_{\epsilon}-\sigma\right\|_{C^{\ell}(\overline{B_{1}})}\,&\leq C\,\|\mu\|_{C^{\ell}(\overline{B_{1}})}\,\left\|\frac{\sigma_{\epsilon}}{\mu}-\frac{\sigma}{\mu}\right\|_{C^{\ell}(\overline{B_{1}})}\\
&=C\,\|\mu\|_{C^{\ell}(\overline{B_{1}})}\,\left\|u_{\epsilon}-f\right\|_{C^{\ell}(\overline{B_{1}})}\\
&\leq C\,\|\mu\|_{C^{\ell}(\overline{B_{1}})}\,\epsilon,\end{split}

(A.11)

which gives (
A.7
), up to renaming
\epsilon
.

Moreover, if
|(x,t)|<1
,

(\sigma_{\epsilon}-\mu u_{\epsilon})u_{\epsilon}=0=D^{\alpha}_{t,a_{\epsilon}}u_{\epsilon}+(-\Delta)^{s}u_{\epsilon},

thanks to (
A.9
), and this proves (
A.5
).

In addition, recalling (
A.11
) and (
A.4
),

u_{\epsilon}=\mu^{-1}\sigma_{\epsilon}\geq\mu^{-1}\sigma-\frac{1}{\inf_{\overline{B_{1}}}\mu}\|\sigma-\sigma_{\epsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{1})}\geq\mu^{-1}\sigma-\frac{\|\mu\|_{C^{\ell}(\overline{B_{1}})}\,\epsilon}{\inf_{\overline{B_{1}}}\mu},

in
B_{1}
, which proves (
A.8
), up to renaming
\epsilon
.
∎
